````text
Course structure AC dated 13.06.2025 pg 203

# Syllabus common for B Tecly MBA Tech (all programs), can use common question paper.

Essential Electronics Practices - Practical based hands on learning

English Communication - This course focuses on introducing the students to the basics of English language and formal communication, which requires to be assessed continuously throughout the semester.

Engineering Ethics - This course focuses on instilling Engineering ethics, which are taught and assessed throughout the semester.

Environmental Studies - The course is for the general awareness about the environment.

Constitution of India - This course focuses on making the students aware of the Constitution of India, which is taught and assessed throughout the semester.
````

````text
SVKM’s Narsee Monjee Institute of Management Studies

Program: | B. Tech. (Information Technology) Semester: | VI
Course: | Advanced Computer Networks Code: BTIT06104
(AY 2017-18
onwards)
Teaching Scheme Evaluation Scheme

Theory
Lecture | Practical | Tutorial | Credit (3 Hrs, Assessment (ICA)
70 Marks) As per Institute Norms (50
Marks)
3 4 1 45 Scaled to Scaled to
70 marks 30 marks

Prerequisite: Computer networks (BTIT05005)

Objectives:

The objective of this course is to familiarize students with the working of internetworks. This
course addresses various protocols used in the internet and how they interact to provide user
services. It also introduces students to advanced topics in computer networking.

Outcomes:
After completion of this course, students will be able to

1. Design IP addressing scheme for given requirements
2. Analyze packet forwarding and compare different routing protocols
3. Compare and analyze transport protocols and key protocols essential for networking
4, Understand various multimedia protocols
5. Understand Internet security concepts
Detailed Syllabus:

Unit | Description Duration

1. | Internet protocol: [Pv4, compare IPv4 and IPv6, IPv6 - (address, special 8
address, extension header, transition from IPv4 to IPv6; subnetting in
classful and classless addressing, NAT

2.| Network Layer: Routing tables and forwarding, Routing protocols - 8
Routing Information Protocol, Open Shortest Path First and Border
Gateway Protocol

3.| Network control protocols: ARP(features, working), DHCP-(working 4

of configuration), ICMP-(ICMPv4, ICMPv6 - features and services)

HOD (IT), MPSTME

comparison
“"~S$-| Multimedia in the internet: Streaming audio/video stored - 8

(characteristics), Streaming audio/video live (characteristics), Streaming
audio/video real-time interactive - (characteristics); real-time interactive
protocols; rationale for new protocols; RTP/RTCP, SIP, SCTP; QoS
(flow classes, flow control to improve QoS - FIFO, Priority and WFQ)

L :

| 6. | Network security: Introduction to Internet security - application layer 6
| security, transport layer security (SSL and TLS), IPSec, VPN, Firewall

~~ 7.| Introduction to Internet of Things and applications D)
````

````text
Date : 04" July, 2011

SVKM’s NMIMS
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering
B.Tech. (Information Technology) Trimester: VI
Sub : Microprocessor & Microcontrollers Code : MPMC
Periods per week Lecture | 5 Hrs
Practical | 2 Hrs
Tutorial | -
Hours Marks
Evaluation Scheme | Theory Examination 3 50 (100 scaled down to
50)
Test 20
Term Work/Practical/Oral 30

Objective: The course objective is to have a good understanding of 8086 architecture. It stresses
on the programming and interfacing aspect. It also aims at covering the integrated approach of a
microcontroller and the study of 8051.

Prerequisite | Digital logic design
Detailed Syllabus
Unit Topics Duration(Hr)
1 | Intel 8086/8088 microprocessor family 07

Features, Architecture and Programmer’s Model of 8086/8088
microprocessor family. Latches 8282, Clock Generator 8284 and
Transceivers 8286. 8086 family minimum and maximum mode
based minimum system. Timing diagram for 8086 family, detailed
study of maximum mode connection: study of 8288 bus controller.

2 | Programming of 8086
Addressing Modes, Study of 8086 Instruction set. Assembly 05
language programming. Assembler Directives, Passing parameters
to Procedure and Macros.

3 | 8086 interrupt structure. 05
Hardware, Software and Program Generated interrupts in 8086.
Response to interrupts. Interrupt Vector Table, Interrupt
Acknowledge machine cycle. 8259 PIC, its block diagram, priority
modes, EOI, and interfacing with 8086.

4 | Programmable Interface and peripheral devices: 06
Programming and applications of 8455/8156
programmable I/O ports and timer, 8255A programmable
peripheral interface , 8253/8254 programmable interval
timer, 8257 direct memory access controller, 8279
programmable keyboard/display interface

5 | Introduction to Multiprocessor systems & 8087 Math Co- 07
processor: Study of architetture of 8087 floating point co-
processor. Data types supported by 8087. Host and co- processor
interface, Assembly language Programming for 8086 - 8087 based
systems. Multiprocessor configurations:- Loosely coupled and
tightly coupled configurations. Study of concept of bus arbitration.
Design of 8086 based multiprocessor systems (without timing
considerations).

1|Page
--- page 2 ---
| ff Date : 04" July, 2011
SVKM’s NMIMS

Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering

6 | Hardware of 8051 Microcontrollers: Comparison of
microprocessor and microcontroller, architecture and pin functions 06
of 8051 single chip microcontroller, CPU timing and machine
cycles, internal memory organization, program counter and stack,
input/output ports, counters and timers serial data input and output
interrupts.
7 | 8051 Assembly Language Programming: Instruction set, 06
addressing modes, Immediate registers, direct and indirect date
movement and exchange instructions, push and pop op — codes,
arithmetic and logic instructions, bit level operations, jump and
call instructions, input/output port programming, programming
timers, asynchronous serial data communication, timer and
hardware interrupt service routines.
8 | Microcontroller Design and Interfacing: External memory and 08
memory address decoding, memory napped L/O, time delay
subroutines, look — up table implementation, interfacing matrix
key board and seven segment displays through scanning and
interrupt driven programs, interfacing analog to digital and
digital to analog converters using handshake signals and waveform
generation interfacing with 8255 Input/Output the parallel printer.
TOTAL 50

Text Books:
1. John Uffenback, 8086 / 8088 Design, Programming and Interfacing, second edition,
ninth Indian reprint, Prentice Hall of India, 2001.
2. Douglas Hall, Microprocessors interfacing and programming, Tata McGraw Hill,
third edition 2006
3. Kenneth Ayala, The 8051 Microcontrollers Architecture, Programming &
Applications, Penram International (India) 2006

Recommended Books:

1. Goankar, Microprocessor Architecture, Programming and applications, Wiley Eastern
1989
2. 5. Muhammad A Mazidi, The 8051 Microcontroller and Embedded Systems, Pearson
Education Asia, first Indian reprint, 2002
’ Additional Reading:
John Uffenback, The 80X86 family Design, Programming and Interfacing, third edition,
Pearson Education Asia, 2002

Internet references: As directed by faculty

Term work consists of the following
1. Minimum two assignments.
2. Minimum 7 practical experiments covering all the topics.
3. Two class tests.

Date : 04"" July, 2011
SVKM’s NMIMS
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering

B.Tech. (Information Technology/Computer) Trimester — VI
Sub : Operating System Code : OS
Periods per week Lecture | 4 Hrs.
Practical | 2 Hrs.
Tutorial | -
Hours Marks
Evaluation Scheme _| Theory Examination 3 50 (100 scaled down to 50)
Test 20
Term Work/Pr. /Oral 30
Objective:

This course is designed to introduce the most fundamental system program which control all
the resources of the computer and provide the base upon which application programs can be
written. Student will learn important resources and their management policies, algorithms used
by operating system. This fundamental will help them to study and design modern operating
systems in subsequent trimester.

Prerequisite: Computer organization & architecture, Microprocessor, programming
languages (c / c++ / Java).

Detailed Syllabus

Unit Topics Duration(Hr)

1 | Operating System Overview 04
Operating system objectives and functions, evolution of operating
system, characteristics of modern operating system, basic
concepts: Processes, files, system calls, shell, layered structure v/s
monolithic structure of OS, introduction to distributed OS, RTOS,
Mobile OS.

2 | Process and Process Scheduling 08
Process description, Process control block, threads, thread
management, comparison between Processes and Threads, process
scheduling :Types, study and comparison of various scheduling
algorithms

3 | Process concurrency 08
Principles of concurrency : mutual exclusion — hardware
approaches, mutual exclusion — software approaches, semaphores,
monitors, message passing, reader/writer problem,
Deadlock and starvation: principles of deadlock, deadlock
prevention, deadlock avoidance, deadlock detection, An integrated
deadlock strategy, dining philosopher problem.

4 | Memory Management 06
Memory management requirements, memory partitioning, virtual
memory management, paging, segmentation, segmented paging,
design and implementation issues in paging and segmentation,
page replacement algorithms, page fault handling, working set
model.

5__| 1/O Management and Disc Scheduling

3|Page ; ae
--- page 4 ---
A Date : 04" July, 2011
SVKM’s NMIMS
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering

I/O devices, organization of the I/O function, operating system
design issues, I/O buffering, disk scheduling and disk scheduling
algorithms, RAID, Disk cache.

6 | File management 04
Overview, file organization, file directories, file sharing record
blocking, secondary storage management. UNIX file system.

7 | Case study 04
Overview of Windows Operating System: Process and thread
management, scheduling, concurrency control mechanisms,
memory management and I/O management.

TOTAL 40

Text Books:

1. William Stallings, “Operating Systems”, 4" Edition, Pearson, 2004R.

2. Abraham Silberschatz, Peter B. Galvin and Greg Gagne, “Operating System
Concepts”, 3 Edition, Wiley, 2009.

3. Ann McHoes, ,”Understanding Operating Systems”, 5"
2007

Edition, Course Technology,

Reference Books:

1. Andrew Tannenbaum, Woodhull Albert, “Operating Systems : Design and
Implementation”, 2"! Edition, PHI, 2002R.

2. Milan Milenkovic, “Operating System”, 2"! Edition, Mc-Graw Hill, 2002R.

3. Andrew S Tanenbaum, “Modern Operating Systems”, Prentice Hall India, 1992.

4. Achyut Godbole; “Operating Systems”, McGraw-Hill, 3“ Edition, 2010

Internet references:

As directed by faculty

B.Tech. (Information Technology) Trimester VI
Sub: Database Management Systems Code : DBMS
Periods per week Lecture | 4 Hrs.
Practical | 2 Hrs.
Tutorial | -
Hours Marks
Evaluation Scheme _| Theory Examination | 3 50 (100 scaled down to 50)
Test 20
Term Work/Pr/Oral 30
Objective:

To provide the knowledge of relational database systems, design their management and
implementation.

Prerequisite: CP —I, II & Data Structure

Detailed Syllabus
````

````text
Objective: To Make student able to understand principles & practice required to develop quality
software, large in size with team. We want to develop ability & skills within student for
Requirement Analysis, design & Modeling. Students will be exposed to Software development
Processes, Practices, Standards & Software Projects Management concepts

Prerequisite | Programming Skills, Software Engineering I

Detailed Syllabus

Unit Duration
Topics (Hr)

1 | Importance of Software Engineering: Role of Software, Categories of 2
Software, Legacy Software, Software Myths

2 | Prescriptive Process Models: Process Framework, Capability Maturity
Model Integration Waterfall Model, Incremental & RAD Models, 6
Prototyping, Spiral Model, Concurrent Development Model

3 Agile Process Models: Agility, Agile Process, Extreme Programming,
Adaptive Software Development, Dynamic Software Development, SCRUM, 4
Crystal

4 | Requirement Analysis & Design: Requirement Engineering tasks,, Elements
of Analysis Model, Data Modeling Concepts, Data Flow Model, Control
Flow Model, Control Specification, Process Specification Design Process &
Design Quality, Design Concepts.

5 | Architectural Design: Software Architecture, Data Design, Architectural
Styles, Representing System in Context, Refining Architecture into 6
‘Components, Mapping Data Flow into a Software Architecture

6 | User Interface Design: Golden Rules for User Interface Design, Interface
|| Analysis & Design, Interface Design Steps

7 | Testing Strategies & Tactics: Test Strategies for Conventional Software,
Validation Testing, System Testing, Black-Box & White-Box Testing, Basis 5
Path Testing, Control Structure Testing.

8 | Software Quality & Metrics: McCall’s Software Quality Factors, ISO 9126
Quality Factors, Framework for Product Metrics, Function-based Matrices,

Architectural Design Metrics, Process & Project Metrics, Size oriented & 6
Function Oriented Metrics, Metrics for Software Quality, SQA Activities
````

````text
Sub: Computer Simulation and Modeling Code : CSM
Periods per week Lecture | 4 Hrs
Practical | 2 Hrs
Tutorial | -
Hours Marks
Evaluation System Theory 3 50 (100 scaled down
Examination to 50)
Test 20
Term Work 30

Objective: The objective of this course is to teach students methods for modeling of systems
using discrete event simulation. Emphasis of the course will be on modeling and on the use of
simulation software. The students are expected to understand the importance of simulation in IT
sector, manufacturing, telecommunication, and service industries etc. By the end of the course
students will be able to formulate simulation model for a given problem, implement the model in
software and perform simulation analysis of the system.

Prerequisite: Probability and Statistics

Detailed Syllabus
Unit Topics Duration(Hr)
1 __| Introduction to Simulation and Modeling: 2
Simulation: appropriate and not appropriate, advantages and
disadvantage, application areas. System and System environment,
Components of system, Type of systems, Model of a system, Type of
models, Steps in simulation study.
2 | Manual Simulation of Systems: 3
Simulation of Queuing Systems such as single channel and multi
channel, lead time demand, Inventory system, reliability problem,
Random normal numbers.
3 | Discrete Event Formalisms: 3
Concepts of discrete even simulation, model components, a discrete
event system simulation. World views or formalisms, simulation of
single channel queue, multi channel queue, inventory system and dump
truck problem using event scheduling approach.
4 | Simulation Software: 2
History of simulation software, An evaluation and selection technique for
simulation software, General — purpose simulation packages, Trends in
simulation software.
W 15 | Statistical and Queueing Models in Simulation: 5
Overview of probability and statistics, Useful statistical model, Discrete
distribution, Continuous distribution, Poisson process, Empirical
distribution.
Characteristics of Queueing systems, Queueing notations, Long run
measures of performance of Queueing Systems, Steady state behavior of
Markovian models, Network of Queues.

analysis and design (OOAD) to tackle a complete OO project. It also gives the thought to acquire
a common language for talking about requirements, designs, and component interfaces. Also
addresses the main principles of good OO design, what major tasks are appropriate to developing
OO models and software, the issues and options in reuse and component based development
````

````text
Objective: The course object would be to present the principles underlying the functioning
of distributed systems, create an awareness of the major technical challenges in distributed
systems design and implementation, expose students to modern and classic technology
used in distributed systems and their software, expose students to past and current research
issues in the field of distributed systems and provide experience in the implementation of
typical algorithms used in distributed systems.

Prerequisite: Operating system, Computer Network

Detailed Syllabus

Unit Topics Duration(Hr)

1 | Introduction to Distributed System 3
Definition, goals, Examples of Distributed system: Internet,
Distributed Computing System, Distributed information system.

2 | Architecture of Distributed System 3
System architecture : centralized architecture, decentralized
architecture, hybrid architecture, architecture versus middleware,

3 Processes 4
Threads, virtualization, clients, servers, code migration
4 | Communication in Distributed System 6

Fundamental, remote procedure call, message oriented
communication, stream oriented communication, multicast
communication, Java RMI case study.

5 | Naming 4
Names, identifiers, and addresses, flat naming, structure naming,
attributed-based naming

6 | Synchronization 6
Clock synchronization, logical clocks, mutual exclusion, global
positioning of nodes, election algorithm.

7 | Consistency and replication 4
Introduction, data centric consistency model, client-centric
consistency models, replica management, fault tolerance services.

8 | Security 3
Secure channels, access control, security management
9 | Distributed file system 3
File service architecture, sun network file system, recent advances
10 | Case study 4
Distributed Object-based system, distributggewelmbased system.

Objectives of the course: Course in Software architecture enables the students to develop
the attitude of architect which is a must in real life software development projects. Students
learn from experience from past project implementations and innovate components,
subsystems and framework to reuse and simplify the designing process. With software
architecture knowledge, they quickly relate the problem definition with most suitable
architectural style from the available solutions.

Pre-requisites:

Objective: Signal processing is one of fundamental theories and techniques to construct
modern information systems. In this course, students will study the fundamentals of
discrete-time signals and systems. The course content includes the concept and the
classification of discrete-time signal, representations of signals in time, frequency, z- and
discrete frequency domains, representations and analyses of systems, and filter designs.

Prerequisite: Principles of Communication and Digital Communication

Unit Topics Duration(Hr)

The Concept of Frequency in Continuous — Time and Discrete —
Time Signals, Analog to Digital and Digital to Analog Conversion

Discrete — Time Linear Time Invariant Systems, Discrete —
Time Systems Described by Difference Equations,
Implementation of Discrete - Time Systems; Correlation of
Discrete — Time Signals

Domain

Applications:

Properties of the DFT; Linear Filtering Methods Based on the
DFT; Frequency Analysis of Signals Using the DFT

Objective:

To develop Application Development Skills

Prerequisite:
Any Programming Language, Database Concepts

Assignment of an application to be developed will be given to each of the students in
the class by the department faculty. Each student will prepare and make a working
application applying their concepts of programming and databases learnt by self study
under the guidance of the faculty. They will prepare a report containing the details of
the application including the design & screenshots. Evaluation will be based on the
report and demonstration of the working model developed. They will be given 30

minutes for demonstration of their working application.

3. System Requirements
4. Database Architecture
5. Database Design
6. Use Case Model
7. Screenshots
8. Future Scope
````

````text
Objective: The course provides grounding in digital filter and transforms techniques for
image processing and feature extraction. An overview of common heuristic algorithms for
image processing. The different representations of digital images, the importance of
adequate sampling frequencies and the appearance of artifacts. Also how the important
features in an image may be related to significant abstractions from the raw image.

Prerequisite: Digital Signal Processing

Detailed Syllabus
° Duration(Hr
Unit Topics ay
1 Digital Image Processing Systems: 2

Introduction, structure of human eye, image formation in the
human eye, Brightness adaptation and discrimination, Image
sensing and acquisition, storage, Processing, Communication,
Display Image Sampling and quantization, Basic relationships
between pixels.

2 Image Transforms (implementation): a
Introduction to Fourier transform, DFT and 2-D DFT, Properties
of 2-D DFT, FFT, IFFT, Walsh transform, Hadamard transform,

h Discrete cosine transform, Slant transform, Optimum transform:

Karhunen — Loeve (Hotelling) transform.

3 Image Enhancement in the Spatial and Frequency Domain: 7
Gray level transformations, Histogram processing, Arithmetic
and logic operations, Spatial filtering: Introduction, Smoothing
and sharpening filters. Frequency domain filters: Smoothing and
Sharpening filters, Homomorphic filtering.

4 Wavelets and Multiresolution Processing: 7
Image pyramids, Subband coding, Haar transform, Series
expansion expansion, Scaling functions, Wavelet functions,
Discrete wavelet transforms in one dimension, Fast wavelet

transform
5 Image Data Compression: ; 7

Objective: The aim of the course is to impart knowledge of the current theories, methods
and techniques in the field of Artificial Intelligence like heuristic search techniques,
knowledge technology, gaming, learning techniques etc. this subject also make student
capable to apply this knowledge to analyze, design and develop Al-systems. They can
formulate scientific questions and is able to solve problems with the aid of abstraction and
modeling.

Prerequisite: Advanced Programming Concepts and Data Structures

Detailed Syllabus

° Duration(Hr)
Unit Topics

1 Introduction to Artificial Intelligence: 4
Definition, the Al problems, physical symbol system, physical
symbol system hypothesis, Intelligent agents: agents and
environment, agent types, desirable properties of knowledge,
Al techniques, different AI techniques.

2 Problems, problem spaces and search: 3
Need of a system to solve a problem, problem as a state space
search, requirement of a formal description of a problem,
production system, control strategy & its requirement, breadth first

h search & depth first search,

13 Heuristic search technique: 6
Heuristic technique, problems of heuristic technique with real
world, different weak methods such as generate and test, hill
climbing, simple hill climbing and steepest ascent hill climbing,
problem with hill climbing such as local maxima, plateau, ridge,
simulated annealing, best first search, OR graphs, A* algorithm,
agenda driven search, AND-OR Graphs, AO* algorithm.

4 Knowledge Representation logic and rules: 7
Predicate logic: facts, representation of fact, mapping between
facts and representation, properties and issues of knowledge
representation system, prepositional logic & predicate logic,
Instance relationship, Isa, gt & It predicate, resolution,conversion |

5 Weak Slot & filler structure: usefulness of slot & filler structure, 6
````

````text
Objective:

The Course objective is to introduce image transforms and its applications, image perception, and
different operations that would be carried out in image analysis. The course also focuses on the
advanced topics like image restoration, steganography techniques, image databases etc.

Prerequisite: Digital Signal Processing and Image Processing

Detailed Syllabus
Unit Topics Duration(Hr)
1 | Basics of Image Processing : Modeling of Image as Random Variable, 6

Mean Variance, Co-Variance matrix and its properties, Eigen vector, K-
L Hotelling Transform, Auto-Correlation, Power Density Spectrum and
Moments

2 | Image Compression : Redundancy in images, Inter-pixel redundancy, 6
coding redundancy, and psycho-visual redundancy. Various Lossy and
lossless techniques to minimize these redundancies

3 | Image Transforms : Kronecker product of matrices and development 6
of Fast Algorithm. Application of Kronecker product algorithm to
Hadamard, Walsh, and Haar Transforms. Similar Fast Algorithms for
DFT, DCT, DST and Slant Transform

4 | Image Perception : Light, Luminance, Brightness and contrast color 6
images, RGB color scheme, CMYK, YCbCr, LUV, HSI and other Color
spaces and their relationship to RGB

5 | Image Analysis : Spatial feature extraction, Edge detection, Boundary 6
Representation , moment representation, Morphological operations:
Dilation, Erosion, Opening and closing. Morphological operations on
Binary and grayscale images

6 | Advanced Topics : Image Restoration, Vector Quantization, Image 10
q Mosaicing, Image Fusion, Digital Watermarking, Steganography and

Information Hiding, Principle Component Analysis and its Applications

to Finger Print and Face Recognition, Image Databases-CBIR.

TOTAL 40
````

````text
Objective: To provide a thorough introduction to "Service Oriented Architecture" (SOA),
which refers to a design pattern made up of components and interconnections that stress
interoperability and location transparency. It includes covers both the design of SOA systems
as well as practical hands-on programming of a distributed Web Service based system.

Prerequisite: Computer Organization, Distributed Computing, Programming Skills

Detailed Syllabus

Unit Topics Duration(Hr)

characteristics, concept of a service in SOA, misperceptions about
SOA, Basic SOA architecture, infrastructure services, Enterprise
Service Bus (ESB), SOA Enterprise Software models. Web Services
and Primitive SOA, SOA Platforms.

; Introduction, XML schema, WSDL language basic, SOAP language

Distributing service management and monitoring concepts, operational
management challenges, Service-level agreement considerations, SOA

Objective: The course aims at giving a practical introduction to parallel programming in C
using MPI. It gives a high-level overview of parallel architectures. The course presents
parallel algorithm design and explains how to apply the design methodology to develop
parallel programs with MPI.

Prerequisite: C Programming, Data Structures and Algorithms, Distributed Systems

Detailed Syllabus

Unit Topics Duration(Hr)

1 | Introduction 4
Evolution of Supercomputing, Modern Parallel Computers,
Seeking concurrency, Data Clustering, Programming Parallel
Computers

2 | Parallel Architectures 4
Introduction, Interconnection Networks, Processor Arrays,
Multiprocessors, Multicomputers, Flynn’s Taxanomy

3 | Parallel Algorithm Design 4
The Task/Channel Model, Foster’s Design Methodology,
Examples of Parallel Algorithm Design

4 | Message Passing Programming *
The Message Passing Model, Interface, Initialization functions,
Collective Communication, Benchmarking Parallel Performance

5 _| Sieve of Eratosthenes a
Introduction, Sequential Algorithm, Sources of Parallelism, Data
Decomposition Options, Developing Parallel Algorithm, Analysis

and Documentation, Improvements

6., | Floyd’s Algorithm 4
The All-Pairs Shortest Path Problem, Creating Arrays at Run-Time,
Designing Parallel Algorithm, Point-to-Point Communication,
Documenting the Parallel Program, Analysis and Benchmarking

7 | Performance Analysis 4
Speedup and Efficiency, Amdahl’s Law, Gustafson-Barsis Law,
The Karp-Flatt Metric, The Isoefficiency Matrix

8 | Matrix-Vector Multiplication 5
Sequential Algorithm, Data Deco Hi tions, Row-wise
Blocked Striped Decompositio: Acchissenees Block Striped

Objective: The objective of this course is to understand the need to carry large volumes of traffic
with different QoS requirements over networks operating at very high data rates. Thus this course
provides a comprehensive, integrated and up-to-date survey of the key issues of high speed TCP/IP
networks, the technology that dominates the field of high-speed networking. The wide range of design
issues related to high-speed networks like ATM, Frame Relay, High Speed LAN’s and Optical
Networks along with congestion control, provision of different levels of quality of service (QoS)
covered.

Prerequisite : Computer Networks, Fundamentals of Internetworking

DETAILED SYLLABUS

Units Topics Duration (hrs.)

1 Network services and layered architectures 2
Application, Traffic characterization and QoS, Network Services, High
performance network, layered architecture, Network architectures ,
Network bottleneck

2 Frame Relay architecture and layers, extended address, FRADs, VOFR, 3
LMI

3 Asynchronous transfer mode - ATM Protocol Architecture, ATM 4
logical Connection, ATM Cell - ATM Service Categories - AAL.

i4 High Speed LANs: Fast Ethernet, Gigabit Ethernet, Fiber Channel, 4
Wireless LANs: applications, requirements - Architecture of 802.11,
' FDDI, DQDB

5 CONGESTION AND TRAFFIC MANAGEMENT: Queuing 4
Analysis- Queuing Models - Single Server Queues - Effects of
Congestion - Congestion Control - Traffic Management - Congestion
Control in Packet Switching Networks - Frame Relay Congestion
Control.

work, Traffic Control - ABR traffic Management - ABR rate control, RM
````

````text
Objective: The course objective is to have a good understanding of 8086
architecture. It stresses on the programming and interfacing aspect. It also aims
at covering the integrated approach of a microcontroller and the study of 8051.

Prerequisite | Digital logic design

Unit Topics Duration(Hr
)
1 | Intel 8086/8088 microprocessor family 07

Features, Architecture and Programmer's Model of
8086/8088 microprocessor family. Latches 8282, Clock
Generator 8284 and Transceivers 8286. 8086 family
minimum and maximum mode based minimum system.
Timing diagram for 8086 family, detailed study of maximum
mode connection: study of 8288 bus controller.

2 | Programming of 8086
Addressing Modes, Study of 8086 Instruction set. Assembly 05
language programming. Assembler Directives, Passing
parameters to Procedure and Macros.

3 | 8086 interrupt structure. 05

8086. Response to interrupts. Interrupt Vector Table,

diagram, priority modes, EOI, and interfacing with 8086.

4 | Programmable Interface and peripheral devices: 06
Programming and applications of 8455/8156
programmable I/O ports and timer, 8255A programmable
peripheral interface , 8253/8254 programmable interval
timer, 8257 direct memory access controller, 8279
programmable keyboard/display interface

5 | Introduction to Multiprocessor systems & 8087 Math Co- 07
processor: Study of architecture of 8087 floating point co-
processor. Data types supported by 8087. Host and co-
processor interface, Assembly language Programming for
8086 - 8087 based systems. Multiprocessor configurations:-
Loosely coupled and tightly coupled configurations. Study of
concept of bus arbitration. Design of 8086 based
multiprocessor systems (without timing considerations).

6 | Hardware of 8051 Microcontrollers: Comparison of
microprocessor and microcontroller, architecture ane pirrTeL s 06

Objective:

This course is designed to introduce the most fundamental system program which
control all the resources of the computer and provide the base upon which application
programs can be written. Student will learn important resources and_ their
management policies, algorithms used by operating system. This fundamental will
help them to study and design modern operating systems in subsequent trimester.

Prerequisite: Computer organization & architecture, Microprocessor, programming
languages (c / c++ / Java).

Detailed Syllabus

Unit Topics Duration(Hr)

1 | Operating System Overview 04
Operating system objectives and functions, evolution of
operating system, characteristics of modern operating
system, basic concepts: Processes, files, system calls, shell,
layered structure v/s monolithic structure of OS, introduction
to distributed OS, RTOS, Mobile OS.

2 | Process and Process Scheduling 08
Process description, Process control block, threads, thread
management, comparison between Processes and Threads,
process scheduling :Types, study and comparison of various
scheduling algorithms

3 | Process concurrency 08
Principies of concurrency : mutual exclusion — hardware
approaches, mutual exclusion — software approaches,
semaphores, monitors, message passing, reader/writer
problem,
Deadlock and starvation: principles of deadlock, deadlock
prevention, deadlock avoidance, deadlock detection, An
integrated deadlock strategy, dining philosopher problem.
````

````text
B.Tech. (Information Technology) Trimester VIII
Sub: Computer Simulation and Modeling Code : CSM
Periods per week Lecture | 4 Hrs
Practical | 2 Hrs
Tutorial | -
Hours Marks
Evaluation System Theory 3 50 (100 scaled down
Examination to 50)
Test 20
Term Work 30

Objective: The objective of this course is to teach students methods for modeling of systems
using discrete event simulation. Emphasis of the course will be on modeling and on the use of
simulation software. The students are expected to understand the importance of simulation in IT
sector, manufacturing, telecommunication, and service industries etc. By the end of the course
students will be able to formulate simulation model for a given problem, implement the model in
software and perform simulation analysis of the system.

Prerequisite: Probability and Statistics

Detailed Syllabus
Unit Topics Duration(Hr)
1 Introduction to Simulation and Modeling: 2

Simulation: appropriate and not appropriate, advantages and
disadvantage, application areas. System and System environment,
Components of system, Type of systems, Model of a system, Type of
models, Steps in simulation study.

2 | Manual Simulation of Systems: 3
Simulation of Queuing Systems such as single channel and multi
channel, lead time demand, Inventory system, reliability problem,
Random normal numbers.

3 | Discrete Event Formalisms: 3
Concepts of discrete even simulation, model components, a discrete
event system simulation. World views or formalisms, simulation of
single channel queue, multi channel queue, inventory system and dump
truck problem using event scheduling approach.

4 | Simulation Software: 2
History of simulation software, An evaluation and selection technique for
simulation software, General — purpose simulation packages, Trends in
simulation software.

5 | Statistical and Queueing Models in Simulation: 5
Overview of probability and statistics, Useful statistical model, Discrete
distribution, Continuous distribution, Poisson process, Empirical
distribution.

Characteristics of Queueing systems, Queueing notations, Long run
measures of performance of Queueing Systems, Steady state behavior of
Markovian models, Network of Queues.

6 | Random Number Generation: 5
Properties ofrandom.oumbers, Generation of truly pseudo random
numbers ‘echniques for enerating random numbers, Hypothesis
--- page 3 ---
SVKM’s
NMIMS Deemed-to-be University
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & Engineering

testing, Tests for uniformity and independence.

Random Variate Generation: 3
Random variate, Different techniques to generate random variate Inverse
transform technique: algorithm, graphical view, and method for discrete

and continuous distribution, Direct transformation technique,
Convolution method, and Acceptance rejection techniques.

Input Modeling: 5
Development steps of a useful model of input data, Data Collection,
Identifying the distribution with Data, Parameter estimation, Suggested
estimators Goodness of fit tests, Selection input model without data,
Covariance and correlation, Multivariate and Time series input models.

Verification and validation of Simulation Model: 4
Model building, Verification of simulation models, Calibration and
validation of models; validation process, face validity, validation of

model, validating input-output transformation, t-test, power of test, Input

output validation using historical data and Turing test.

10

Output Analysis for a Single Model: 8
Types of simulations with respect to output analysis, Stochastic nature of

output data, Measure of performance and their estimation, Output

analysis of terminating simulators, output analysis for steady state
simulation.

TOTAL 40

BOOKS

Reference Books:

1. Banks, J., Carson, J. S., Nelson, B. L., and Nicol, D. M. Discrete Event System -,
Simulation, 3rd edition, Pearson Education, 2001. [

2. Gordon, G., System Simulation, 2nd edition, PHI, 1978. 4,

3. Narsing Deo, System Simulation with Digital Computer, PHI. 4;

4. Law, A. M., and Kelton, W. D., Simulation Modeling and Analysis, 3rd edition,
McGraw-Hill, 2000. 3

5. Trivedi, K. S., Probability ‘and Statistics with Reliability, Queueing, and Computer
Science Applications, PHI, 1982.

6. Wadsworth, G. P., and Bryan, J. G., Introduction to Probability and Random Variables,
McGraw-Hill, 1960.

Internet references:
As suggested by faculty.

Term work consists of the following

1. Minimum 7 practical experiments covering all the topics.
2. Two term test papers.

Objective: The main objective of the course is to gain enough competence in object-oriented
analysis and design (OOAD) to tackle a complete OO project. It also gives the thought to acquire
a common language for talking about requirements, designs, and component interfaces. Also
addresses the main principles of good OO design, what major tasks are appropriate to developing
OO models and software, the issues and options in reuse and component based development

Prerequisite: System Analysis and Design

Detailed Syllabus

Unit Topics Duration(Hr)

il Introduction: 4
Object oriented approach, Object oriented themes, and Object
oriented methodologies, Overview of OOL, Object classes; Meta
Types, Object Oriented Methodologies, the Uniform Approach
Modeling; Need of Modeling, Static and Dynamic Models,
Functional Models.

2 Object Modeling: 6

Modeling concepts, Modeling techniques, Objects and classes,
Links and association, multiplicity, Advanced link and association
concepts, Generalization and inheritance, Grouping constructs,
Aggregation, Abstract classes, Generalization as an extension and
restriction, Multiple inheritance, Metadata, Candidate key,
Constraints, Homomorphism, problems on object modeling and
Advanced Object Modeling, Advantages of Object Modeling.

3 Analysis: 4
Problem Analysis, Problem Domain Classes, Identity classes,
Object of Real World Problems using use case analysis and
Recording Analysis.

4 Dynamic Modeling: 6
Events, Modeling scenarios, Mapping Events to Object, Interface,
Discovering attributes scenarios and event trace diagrams,
Modeling simple collaboration, Modeling Logical Database
schema, Activity Diagram, Modeling workflow, Advanced
Dynamic Modeling concepts, Relation of object and dynamic
models.

5 Class and State Diagram: 4
Test “scenarios, Interfaces, classes, Methods, Stress Testing,
/4System> Testing, Scalability Testing, and Regression Testing.

Objective: The objective of this course is to familiarize students with the working of
internetwork. This course looks at various protocols used in the Internet.

Prerequisite | Computer Networks, basic probability theory, programming

Detailed syllabus

Unit Topics Duration (Hr)

1 Introduction and Overview: The motivation For Internetworking, OSI 03
model, The TCP/IP protocol suite, IP addressing (Classful and classless)

Internet Protocol: Virtual Circuit and Datagram networks, [Pv4 header
2 format, fragmentation, checksum and options, IPv6, IPv6 addresses, 04
special addresses, header format, extension headers, IPvé6 transition.

UDP and TCP: Process to Process communication, UDP header format
and operation, TCP services and operations, flow control, silly window
syndrome, error control, TCP timers, congestion control, segment,
options, checksum, state transition diagram.

ARP, RARP and DHCP: ARP and RARP packet format, encapsulation
4 | and operation, proxy ARP, DHCP packet format, DHCP messages and 02
state transition diagram.

Delivery and routing of IP packets: Direct and indirect delivery,
routing methods (next hop routing, network specific routing, host
specific routing, default routing) static routing and dynamic routing,
routing table, routing in classful and classless address environment.

Unicast Routing Protocols: RIPv] and v2 message format, timers,
problems in RIP and remedies, OSPF basic concept, OSPF operation,

6 OSPF packets and packet formats, path vector routing, BGP packets and sa
packet formats.
Packet queues and delay Analysis: little’s theorem, birth and death
7 | process, queueing disciplines, Markovian FIFO queueing system (M/M/1 06
only) KN
````

````text
Objective: At the end of the course, students should be able to understand how to build
Embedded Systems software. Students undergoing this course will understand the hardware
interfacing, sensors, memory programming with respect to popular microcontrollers.

Prerequisite: Operating system, Computer Network

Detailed Syllabus
Unit Topics Duration(Hr)
1 | Introduction to Embedded Systems: Embedded Systems, 2
Categories, Specialties, Application areas, Recent Trends
2 | Architecture of Embedded Systems: Hardware Architecture, 4

Software Architecture, Application Software, Process of Generating
Executable Image, Develpoment / Testing Tools.

3 | Processes 4
Threads, virtualization, clients, servers, code migration

4 | Process of Embedded System Development: The Development 4
Process, Design, Implementation, Integration and Testing

5 Survey of Software Architectures: Round-Robin, Round-Robin with 4

Interrupts, Function-Queue Scheduling Architectures, Real Time
Operating System Architecture, Selecting Architecture.

6 | Introduction to Real-Time Operating System Concepts: Task and 5
Task States, Semaphores, Mutex, Mailboxes, Message Queues, Pipes,
b Signals, Timers, Memory Management, Priority Inversion Problem.

7 ~+| Basic Design Using a Real-Time Operating System: Overview, 5
Principles, An Example, Encapsulating Semaphores and Queues, Hard
» Real-Time Scheduling Considerations, Saving Memory Space, Saving
Power.

8 | Embedded System Programming—I: Embedded C Programming, 6
Operations On Bits, Introduction to Embedded Systems, Embedded
System Boards, First Embedded System Program, Blinking LEDs.

9 |Embedded System Programming—II: EEPROM Programming, 6
Flash Programming, Programming With Sensors, Interrupts & Timers,
Serial Communication, Serial Communication Using Interrupts,
Building Interactive Embedded Systems, Working With RTC

TOTAL 40 |

Objective:

The Course objective is to introduce image transforms and its applications, image perception, and
different operations that would be carried out in image analysis. The course also focuses on the
advanced topics like image restoration, steganography techniques, image databases etc.

’ Prerequisite: Digital Signal Processing and Image Processing
Detailed Syllabus
Unit Topics Duration(Hr)
7 1 | Basics of Image Processing : Modeling of Image as Random Variable, 6

Mean Variance, Co-Variance matrix and its properties, Eigen vector, K-
L Hotelling Transform, Auto-Correlation, Power Density Spectrum and
Moments

2 | Image Compression : Redundancy in images, Inter-pixel redundancy, 6
coding redundancy, and psycho-visual redundancy. Various Lossy and
lossless techniques to minimize these redundancies

3 | Image Transforms : Kronecker product of matrices and development 6
of Fast Algorithm. Application of Kronecker product algorithm to
Hadamard, Walsh, and Haar Transforms. Similar Fast Algorithms for
DFT, DCT, DST and Slant Transform

: 4 | Image Perception : Light, Luminance, Brightness and contrast color 6
» images, RGB color scheme, CMYK, YCbCr, LUV, HSI and other Color
spaces and their relationship to RGB

5 | Image Analysis : Spatial feature extraction, Edge detection, Boundary 6
Representation , moment representation, Morphological operations:
Dilation, Erosion, Opening and closing. Morphological operations on

- Binary and grayscale images

6 | Advanced Topics : Image Restoration, Vector Quantization, Image 10
Mosaicing, Image Fusion, Digital Watermarking, Steganography and
Information Hiding, Principle Component Analysis and its Applications
to Finger Print and Face Recognition, Image Databases-CBIR.

Objective: The course object would be to present the principles underlying the functioning
of distributed systems, create an awareness of the major technical challenges in distributed
systems design and implementation, expose students to modern and classic technology
used in distributed systems and their software, expose students to past and current research
issues in the field of distributed systems and provide experience in the implementation of
typical algorithms used in distributed systems.

w Prerequisite: Operating system, Computer Network
Detailed Syllabus
Unit Topics Duration(Hr)
S 1 | Introduction to Distributed System 3

Definition, goals, Examples of Distributed system: Internet,
Distributed Computing System, Distributed information system.

~ 2 | Architecture of Distributed System 3
System architecture : centralized architecture, decentralized
architecture, hybrid architecture, architecture versus middleware,

3 Processes 4
Threads, virtualization, clients, servers, code migration
4 | Communication in Distributed System 6

Fundamental, remote procedure call, message oriented
communication, stream oriented communication, multicast
communication, Java RMI case study.

Y attributed-based naming

: 6 | Synchronization 6
Clock synchronization, logical clocks, mutual exclusion, global

& positioning of nodes, election algorithm.

7 | Consistency and replication 4
Introduction, data centric consistency model, client-centric
consistency models, replica management, fault tolerance services.

8 | Security 3
Secure channels, access control, security management

9 | Distributed file system 3
File service architecture, sun network file system, recent advances

10 | Case study 4

Distributed Object-based system, distributed web-based system.
````

````text
Objective:

The course provides detailed knowledge, practical training, and insight into the implementation
and management of various storage technologies with a focus towards applying these
technologies in an information lifecycle paradigm.

Prerequisite:
Operating Systems, Computer Networks
Detailed Syllabus
Unit Topics Duration(Hr)
1 | Introduction to Storage Technology: Data proliferation and the 5

varying value of data with time & usage, Sources of data and states of
data creation, Data center requirements and evolution to accommodate
storage needs, Overview of basic storage management skills and
activities, The five pillars of technology, Overview of storage
infrastructure components, Evolution of storage, Information Lifecycle
Management concept, Data categorization within an enterprise, Storage
and Regulations

2 | Storage Systems Architecture: Intelligent disk subsystems overview, 7
Contrast of integrated vs. modular arrays, Component architecture of
intelligent disk subsystems, Disk physical structure- components,

properties, performance, and specifications, Logical partitioning of
disks, RAID & parity algorithms, hot sparing, Physical vs. logical disk
organization, protection, and back end management, Array caching
properties and algorithms, Front end connectivity and queuing
properties, Front end to host storage provisioning, mapping, and
operation, Interaction of file systems with storage, Storage system
connectivity protocols ;

3 | Introduction to Networked Storage : JBOD, DAS, SAN, NAS, & 7
CAS evolution, Direct Attached Storage (DAS) environments:
elements, connectivity, & management, Storage Area Networks (SAN):
elements & connectivity, Fibre Channel principales, standards, &
network management principales, SAN management principles,
Network Attached Storage (NAS): elements, connectivity options,
connectivity protocols (NFS, CIFS, ftp), & management principles, IP

| Information Storage Management, Trimester 10, B.Tech. (Information Technology)
--- page 2 ---
SVKM’s NMIMS University
Mukesh Patel School of Technology Management & E\.

SAN elements, standards (iSCSI, FCIP, iFCP), connectivity principle,
security, and management principles, Content Addressable Storage
(CAS): elements, connectivity options, standards, and management
principles, Hybrid Storage solutions overview including technologies
like virtualization & appliances.

4 | Introduction to Information Availability: Business Continuity and 5
Disaster Recovery Basics, Local business continuity techniques,
Remote business continuity techniques, Disaster Recovery principles &
techniques

5 |Managing & Monitoring: Management philosophies (holistic vs. 8

system & component), Industry management standards (SNMP, SMI-S,
CIM), Standard framework applications, Key management metrics
(thresholds, availability, capacity, security, performance), Metric
analysis methodologies & trend analysis, Reactive and pro-active
management best practices, Provisioning & configuration change
planning, Problem reporting, prioritization, and handling techniques,
Management tools overview

6 | Security & Virtualization: Storage Security (Importance of 8

Information security, elements and attributes of security), Developing a
storage security model (Restricting Access Path, Vulnerability
Management, Understanding Vulnerabilities), Securing Data Storage
(Storage Security domains, Risk assessment Methodology, Security
elements, threats against applications, Controlling user access to data,
threats again backup , recovery and archive)
Virtualization (Define virtualization, types of virtualization), Storage
Virtualization (Storage functionality, Virtual storage, Comparison of
virtualization architectures, challenges of storage virtualization), Block
level virtualization, File level virtualization

Total 40

Recommended Books

Text Books
1. Marc Farley Osborne, “Building Storage Networks”, Tata McGraw Hill
2. Robert Spalding, “Storage Networks: The Complete Reference“, Tata Mcgraw Hill

Reference Reading

1. NIT, “Introduction to Information Security Risk Management” , Prentice-Hall of
India.

2 Information Storage Management, Trimester 10, B.Tech. (Information Technology)
````

````text
Objective: This course is an introduction to the field of Information Security. This course presents

a balance of the managerial and the technical aspects of the discipline and addresses knowledge
areas as specified in the CISSP (Certified Information Systems Security Professional) certification.
Students will learn about the threats to information and ways to over it.

Prerequisite: Operating system, Computer Networks.

DETAILED SYLLABUS
Units -_ Topics a _ | Duration
(Hrs)
1 Introduction: Basic Components — of Computer   security(CIA), 5
Characteristics of Information, vulnerabilities, threats , Attacks and controls
goals of security, NSTISSC security model, Security System development
life cycle, Computer criminals, Internet Standards and RFC.
2 Design Principles: Various Security attacks, method of defense, Design 4
Principles, Security policies, types of security policies
3 Cryptography: Cryptography basics, transposition ciphers, substitution 5

ciphers, DES, Public key cryptography, streams and block ciphers, Key
Management, Digital Signature. PEM and IPSec.

4 Program Security: Secure programs, Non malicious Program Errors, 4
Viruses and other malicious code, types of viruses, attack mechanism of
viruses, Targeted Malicious Code, Controls Against Program Threats.

5 Authentication: Authentication basics, Password, Challenge response, 2
SSO, Biometrics _ -

6 Access Control:, Access control principles, ACL, DAC, MAC, and Role 4
based Access Control, Access control models, Kerberos

| Firewall, Kinds of Firewalls, Filtering Services; DMZ, Implementing 7

policies (Default allow, Default Deny) on proxy, IDS, types of IDS, Virtual
Private Network

8 Physical and Environmental Security: Introduction, Physical security 2
Threats and measures
9 | Risk Management and Business Continuity planning: 4 |

Risk analysis, various terminologies associated with risk management, Risk
assessment techniques, managing risk, steps for risk management, Business
impact analysis, various terminologies associated with BIA, Different types

of continuity planning, testing and revising the plan
````

````text
Objective: Develop an understanding of the Project management & learning the process
of managing projects. Students will become conversant with various tools & techniques
used in project management in modern industrial environment.

Prerequisite | Basic engineering subjects.
Detailed Syllabus
Unit Topics Duration(Hr)
1 | Introduction to Project Management 05

What is PM, Need for PM, Three primary areas of PM

( Time/Schedule, cost & scope), Types of Project Undertaken,
Typical Project Life Cycle (Initiation, Planning, Execution &
Control/Closure)

2 | Project Feasibility Management
Feasibility Study, Pre- feasibility from technical, financial and 05
other perspectives (Various Clearances) Risk analysis, Detailed
Project, Feasibilty Report

3 | Project Scope Management 07
Detailed Scope Document, WBS
4 | Financial Aspects in PM 07

Various estimates, Project Cost, budget, sources of finances,
introduction to ROR, ROI, NPV, BEP, Pay Back Period

5 | Project Schedule Management 05
PERT, CPM, GANTT Charts, LOB.
6 | Project Execution Management 05

Material Management (Procurement, storage, handling &
disposal), Man power Management (types of Contractors/
consultants, their role and selection), types of contracts, Project
Team Structures.

7 | Project Control Management 05
Time and cost control, tools & techniques, Budgetary Controls,
Fund Flow Controls, Project Quality Control

8 | Project Review & Closure 03
Deployment of the project outcome. =
--- page 9 ---
9 | Role of leadership & communication skill 05
Team building, motivation, conflict management, incentives &
rewards, role of documentation, types of communication channels
(formal & informal)

10 | Role of IT in PM 03
CADD, CAM, MS Projects, Primavera
Total 50
````

````text
Objective: The Introduction to Cloud computing (ICC) course educates students about cloud
deployment and service models, cloud infrastructure, and the key considerations in migrating to
cloud computing. The course covers technologies required to build classic (traditional), virtualized,
and cloud data center environments. These technologies include compute, storage, networking,
dgsktop and application virtualization. Additional areas of focus are backup/recovery, business
continuity, security, and management. Students will learn about the key considerations and steps
involved in transitioning from the current state of a data center to a cloud computing environment.
(¢pon completing this course, students will have the knowledge to make informed decisions about
migrating to cloud infrastructure and choosing the best deployment model for an organization.

Prerequisite: Information Storage Management (ISM), Operating System, Computer Systems
and Architecture, Computer networks.

~ DETAILED SYLLABUS
````
